## Context

- G6: hostile input gives SyntaxError or RangeError, never an abort. JS recursion must throw a RangeError, so every interpreter and JIT prologue, every recursive Rust builtin and the parser need a stack check [R09].
- D14: termination comes from another thread. [R18]: an inspector session can run JS at any interrupt poll, so polls are also lazy-deopt points (H24, H29).
- Depends on: H14 (frames), H15 (dispatch), H28 (platform mechanism). Depended on by: H24 (debugger polls), H29 (deopt at polls), H32.

## Decision

Limit checks on VM and native stacks: calls, JIT and AOT prologues, recursive builtins, the 3-layer depth guard. Interrupt polls shared with safepoints and tier-up. Embedder interrupt callbacks never run JS; only an inspector session runs JS at a poll. Heap limit + callback. `panic = "abort"` in release; never unwind panics through JIT or AOT frames (02 §6).

**Facts (as built; first written at `dabc38a`, rows updated through M0 month 2 P4, all checked at `0c4c70e`; the native stack, interrupt poll and termination rows checked again at `bfd5cd8`).**

| Native stack margin | JS entries and host calls stop at stack low end + clamp(size / 4, 128 KiB, 1 MiB): catchable RangeError. | Interrupt poll | one budget unit per `JumpLoop` back edge, per call and per entry from Rust of a bytecode function (a call of a host function that is not bound takes no unit: `call_host` returns before the decrement, `crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs:425-437`, and the T0 call path takes none for a host callee, `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/call.rs:239-254`), per level of a bound function's chain at each call or construct of the bound function that reaches `bound::unwrap`, whatever its target (`bound::unwrap`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/bound.rs:162-167`, reached from `call::call_with` at `crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs:359`, `call::construct_with` at `crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs:630` and `slow::call` at `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/call.rs:619`; so a call of a bound host function takes one unit per bound level), per context of the dynamic lookup walk (`poll`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/lookup.rs:136-143`, M2-P2-POLL), per pass of a builtin loop with neither (`Cx::poll_interrupt`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/index.rs:88-99`; for example each key loop of the Object builtins, M2-P4-BOBJ-POLL), per 256 code units that a string search compares (`SearchWork`, `crates/ep9_builtins/src/string.rs:344`; M0 month 2 P4 review fix S1) and per job of a microtask checkpoint (`crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/jobs.rs:253`); a poll runs when the budget of 10,000 runs out. [2026-10-27, M4-P4 fixer C: also one unit per FinalizationRegistry cleanup callback (`crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/weakref.rs:246`, P4-ORCH-8) and per step of a prototype-chain walk (`prototype_walk_step`, the walk row below). The instanceof cache's hit walk (`instance_ic::hit`) does not take a unit per step: it takes 8 units after each full group of 8 steps, and a walk that returns inside a group (a match, the end of the chain, a proxy) takes no unit for that group, so the last 1 to 8 steps of a walk (all of a walk of 8 steps or fewer) take none. It does not poll: when the budget is used up after a group it misses, and the generic walk polls at its first step (Codex B1-1). So a hit walk that uses up the budget runs up to 7 steps past it, or 8 when the budget is already at or below 0 when it starts (a call from Rust takes its unit after its poll and can leave 0, `crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs:496`). At most 8 steps of a walk go uncharged, and the loop that runs the instanceof polls at its back edge (Codex CX-C-D1).] [2026-10-28, M4-P5 modes fixer; MODES-CHECK-w16 MC-w16-5: the group size 8 (`WALK_STEPS`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/instance_ic.rs`) is a speed value and a tuning value in the doc 13 C-25 form, which freezes at the A4 gate, not with this row at M1 exit. The rule is: at most one group of steps per walk goes uncharged (8 today), and a walk that uses up the budget misses after at most one group past it, so the generic walk polls.] T0's call path `slow::call` also polls, with no unit, when a call starts with the budget already at or below 0 (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/call.rs:58-60`), whatever the callee. Every T0 call takes 1 unit (M3-P2 fix round 2, X-PF1). `call_fast` takes its unit (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/ops.rs:476`) after all of its cold exits except two (M3-P2 PERF3). A T0 call that `call_fast` sends cold before that decrement takes 1 unit if it gets to a later decrement: a sloppy callee with a primitive receiver (H14-Q2) and the call that reaches the feedback threshold take it in `slow::call` at the frame push (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/call.rs:322`), a callee of another realm in `call::call_function_object` at the frame push, and, under spike-native, a (b) callee in `enter_b_cold` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/call.rs:600`) and a Baseline or AotBaseline callee in `enter_native` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/native.rs:238`). The two cold exits after the decrement give the unit back (`budget_out`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/ops.rs:545`): the call whose `call_fast` unit uses up the budget, and a call whose frame does not fit (a full VM stack). The cold path then takes 1 unit at the frame push, whether the push succeeds or fails (M3-P2 fix round 3, R3-2). `slow::call` takes the unit before it checks the frame push of a call (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/call.rs:322`) and of a `new` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/call.rs:195` for the construct stub, `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/call.rs:216` for the frame). Under spike-native, `enter_b_cold` does the same (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/call.rs:600`). So the RangeError of a full VM stack comes after the decrement, and caught overflows use up the budget as other calls do. `slow::call` polls first if the budget is at or below 0. At a budget of 1 it does not poll, the call leaves the budget at 0, and the next unit taker polls, so the poll comes at most 1 unit late. This also holds when every call overflows. The spike-native `call_fast` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/ops.rs:625`) has the same order and gives the unit back at both exits after its decrement, for every slot kind. `enter_native` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/native.rs:206`) takes its unit before it checks the frame push (`crates/ep9_vm/src/native.rs:238`), as `slow::call` does, so a call that gets to `enter_native` takes 1 unit, whether its push succeeds or fails (M3-P2 fix round 5, D1). From T0, such a call goes from `slow::call` through `native_entry` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/call.rs:364`; a `Wide` or `ExtraWide` call through `call_native`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/call.rs:524`) to `enter_native`. Examples: an AotBaseline callee at either `call_fast` exit after the decrement (the call whose unit uses up the budget, and a frame that does not fit); a sloppy AotBaseline callee with a primitive receiver (a number, boolean, string or symbol), which `call_fast` sends cold before its decrement (`call::try_bind_this` at `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/ops.rs:618` comes before the decrement for every slot kind); and any callee in a Baseline slot (`call_fast` sends it cold before its decrement). An AotBaseline callee whose frame fits in `call_fast` takes its unit there and does not get to `enter_native` (the `OP_ENTER_A_HOT` stub calls its native code). So under spike-native, caught overflows of these calls use up the budget as other calls do, and a queued interrupt runs. The other callers of `enter_native` (`call_any` from native code, `call_from_rust`, and `call_in_its_realm` for a native-slotted callee of another realm) get the same rule. Before fix round 5, `enter_native` took its unit only after a frame push that succeeded, and `call_fast` kept its unit only for an AotBaseline callee whose frame did not fit in `call_fast` (that call took 1 unit), so the other overflows in the examples took 0 units (D1). For the AotBaseline examples this was an M3-P2 regression (at cbae45d each took 1 unit); a Baseline-slot callee took 0 units before M3-P2 too. A call that goes cold before the `call_fast` decrement takes 0 units if it throws before a later decrement: a class constructor called without `new` (of this realm or of another realm), a callee that is not callable, a `new` of a value that is not a constructor, and a slot that is not Interp in a build without spike-native (not implemented) (X-R2-2). A call from Rust whose frame does not fit (a full VM stack) takes 1 unit, as other calls do: `call_bytecode` (in `call::call_function_object`; `crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs:496`) and, under spike-native, `run_b` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/native.rs:1126`; `call_from_rust`, `call_any` and `call_in_its_realm` for a (b) callee) take the unit before they check the frame push, as `enter_native` does. So a callee of another realm when the VM stack is full takes 1 unit too, a T0, (b) or native-slotted one (M3-P4 fix round 1, M3-P2-STOP). Before, `call_bytecode` and `run_b` took the unit only after a push that succeeded, so such an overflow took 0 units. In general, a unit is taken only where the call reaches a decrement or `bound::unwrap`; a call that throws earlier takes no unit for the step it did not reach. For example, `new` of a bound function whose target is not a constructor throws in `slow::call` and in `call::construct_with` before `bound::unwrap`, so its bound levels take no unit (Codex round 3). JIT code reaches the same poll through `RuntimeTable::poll` (`rt_poll`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/native.rs:343`). Every poll runs `slow::interrupt` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/mod.rs:130`): embedder callbacks, then termination [2026-11-06, M7-P0 SPEC-POST (fill; MC-SPEC-BIGPOLL):] Long BigInt work takes units too: one unit per `POLL_WORDS` (64) digits of 64 bits (`crates/ep9_bigint/src/lib.rs:70`). `ep9_bigint` takes a poll callback (`Work`, `crates/ep9_bigint/src/lib.rs:74`; it does not depend on `ep9_vm`, dep-guards check 17), and `ep9_vm` gives one that calls `Cx::poll_interrupt` (`poller`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/bigint.rs:126`). Every operator, `asIntN` and `asUintN` takes the units of its operands before the work and of its result before the cell is allocated (`charge`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/bigint.rs:133`). `**`, StringToBigInt and `toString` do long work in steps; they take the units and poll between the steps. StringToBigInt also polls in its text scans: the white-space trim, the ASCII check, the digit check and the leading-zero skip take one unit per 4096 code units [M7-P0 fixer r2, Codex r1 SPEC-POST-2]. A poll that finds a termination stops the work: no RangeError and no result. A value of up to 64 digits takes no unit. The 64 is a T0 count of the part "tuning (gated)", as the 256 code units of a string search step. Gaps: one dashu call (one step) does not poll inside it; nor do the flatten and the UTF-16 copy of a StringToBigInt text (linear in its length; 330,000,000 chars: about 226 ms, Facts row "BigInt size"). The longest are `x * x` of a 2^29-bit `x` (about 4.4 s) and the division of a 2^30-bit value by a 2^29-bit value (about 16 s); measured 2026-11-06 at the M7-P0 base, Apple M5 Max, release build. An error message that names a BigInt above 100 digits of 64 bits shows `<a very large BigInt>` (V8's text; `DECIMAL_MAX_WORDS`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/bigint.rs:203`), so a message does no long work. A thrown BigInt that no script catches (the embedder `Exception` text) shows all its digits, as d8 does: `Isolate::describe` uses `toString`, which polls [M7-P0 fixer r2, reviewer R2] | `BUDGET` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/mod.rs:71`); interrupt bits `TERMINATE` = 1, `REQUEST` = 2 (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/mod.rs:175`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/mod.rs:178`); [2026-11-06, M7-P0 SPEC-POST:] `ep9_vm` `tests::bigint::long_bigint_work_takes_one_budget_unit_per_poll_words` and `tests::bigint::long_bigint_work_stops_on_a_termination`; `ep9_builtins` `tests/bigint.rs` `a_huge_to_string_2_stops_on_a_termination` and `an_interrupt_callback_runs_inside_long_bigint_work`; `ep9_bigint` `tests/ops.rs` `long_work_polls_once_per_poll_words` and `a_false_poll_stops_long_work` |
| Embedder interrupt callback | `InterruptHandle::request(Box<dyn FnOnce() + Send>)` from any thread queues the callback (a `Mutex` queue on the `Runtime`, beside the interrupt word) and sets `REQUEST`; the next poll clears the bit, then runs the queue in request order on the isolate thread. The callback gets no `Cx`: no JS, no JS-heap allocation. It may call `TerminateHandle::terminate` (the same poll stops the script). An idle isolate runs it at its next poll. The handle holds the queue weakly (the `Runtime` owns it): callbacks still queued when the isolate is dropped are dropped with it, unrun, even one that holds a handle; a later request drops its callback at once | `InterruptHandle` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/isolate.rs:205`), `Runtime::run_interrupt_requests` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/runtime.rs:454`); tests `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/isolate_api.rs:177`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/isolate_api.rs:253` |
| Termination | `TerminateHandle` from any thread; the unwinder runs no handler, so no `catch` and no `finally`; the isolate stays usable. The end of a terminated run also drops the microtask job queue, so no job queued before the termination runs at a later run, call or checkpoint (M0 month 2 P4 review fix) | `crates/ep9_vm/src/error.rs:316-323`; `end_termination` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/isolate.rs:895`); tests `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/exceptions.rs:408`, `a_terminated_run_or_call_drops_its_queued_jobs` (`ep9_vm` `tests::jobs`) |
| Heap out of memory | after one collection, a full sweep of both spaces and the near-limit callback, `Runtime::alloc` panics "ep9: heap limit reached"; release builds abort (`panic = "abort"`). Never a JS exception. Shape and feedback-vector cells take the same heap steps, then panic with their own text ("ep9: shape allocation failed", "ep9: heap allocation failed") | `alloc_failed` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/runtime.rs:533`); `crates/ep9_vm/src/shape.rs:1196`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/feedback.rs:165` |
| Heap limit | `HeapConfig::max_heap_bytes` (committed bytes, shape region included); default `usize::MAX`, so the 64 GiB cage (`CAGE_ALIGN`) bounds the heap. `HeapConfig::near_limit: Option<fn(committed, limit) -> usize>`: an allocation that finds no memory calls it once; a higher result becomes the limit (`Heap::max_heap_bytes`) and the allocation retries once | `crates/ep9_heap/src/heap.rs:157`, `crates/ep9_heap/src/heap.rs:113`, `near_limit_raised` (`crates/ep9_heap/src/heap.rs:891`); `crates/ep9_vm/src/shape.rs:220` |
| String length | 2^29 - 24 code units (as V8); concat past it gives RangeError "Invalid string length" | `crates/ep9_vm/src/string.rs:107`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/conv.rs:520` |
| Array length (v0, dense) | 2^26; a larger `length` gives RangeError "Invalid array length" (H18) | `crates/ep9_vm/src/object.rs:288` |
| Front-end depth caps | `AST_CAP` 20,000; `PRESCAN_CAP` 25,000; depth cap + 1 gives RangeError | `crates/ep9_frontend/src/depth.rs:29`, `crates/ep9_frontend/src/depth.rs:37` |
| Proxy forwarding and prototype walks (M4-P4 fixer B; Codex PROXY1 P-1, P-2) | A proxy internal method that forwards to its target holds one level of a per-thread depth until the target's method returns (never a tail call): past `MAX_FORWARD_DEPTH` (10,000) nested forwards, RangeError "Maximum call stack size exceeded" (node v24.22.1 answers `'x' in p` through 4,000 nested proxies and throws at 8,000). Each step of a prototype-chain walk (instanceof, for-in, `isPrototypeOf`, `__lookupGetter__`, `__lookupSetter__`) takes one interrupt budget unit and polls when the budget runs out; past `MAX_PROXY_HOPS` (100 × 1024, V8's `JSProxy::kMaxIterationLimit`) proxies on one walk, the same RangeError. An ordinary chain is never cut (only a proxy can close a cycle). JS can make a proxy only from PROXY2 (month 5) | `crates/ep9_vm/src/proxy.rs:208`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/property.rs:1225`, `prototype_walk_step` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/property.rs:1240`); `ep9_vm` `tests::proxy` |
| FinalizationRegistry cleanup drain (M4-P4 fixer C, P4-ORCH-8; replaces the fixer B row "cleanup rounds") | a microtask checkpoint serves cleanup until none is pending, with no round cap: a GC in a callback that finds more dead targets starts another round. Each cleanup callback (JS or builtin) takes one interrupt budget unit and polls before it runs, as a job does, so the interrupt budget bounds a callback chain that never ends as it bounds a JS loop; a termination stops it, and the cells not yet served stay pending for a later checkpoint | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/weakref.rs:246`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/jobs.rs:247`; `ep9_builtins` tests `a_chain_of_30_cleanup_callbacks_runs_in_one_checkpoint`, `a_termination_stops_a_cleanup_chain_and_the_rest_runs_later`, `a_builtin_cleanup_callback_polls_for_interrupts` |
| Release panic strategy | abort: `panic = "abort"` in `[profile.release]` (M0 month 2 P2). `dev-opt` inherits it, so every release and `dev-opt` artifact aborts on a panic: ep9, ep9-test262, ep9-bench, the disasm-check and fp-check builds. The dev profile (`cargo build`, `cargo test`) unwinds; test harnesses, build scripts and proc macros always do. A bin that a test runs through `CARGO_BIN_EXE_*` aborts under `cargo test --release` | root `Cargo.toml` lines 87-97; dep-guards check 13 |
| Code that catches panics (dev profile only: under `panic = "abort"` a panic never unwinds to a catch) | test262 exec and parse, inside their worker processes; the front-end worker; the `ep9` join arm (a fallback: the panic hook exits first) | `tools/test262/src/exec.rs:974`, `tools/test262/src/parse.rs:256`, `crates/ep9_frontend/src/worker.rs:93`, `crates/ep9_cli/src/run.rs:471-474` |
| Panic isolation that needs no `catch_unwind` (as built, M2-P1-ABORT-PRE) | test262 parse and exec run every file in worker processes. A worker that ends (abort, signal, or any panic under `panic = "abort"`) is a `worker crash` infrastructure error, exit 2, and the report line carries the panic text: the worker panic hook writes it to stderr. `ep9`'s panic hook prints `ep9: internal error: <msg> (this is an ep9 bug)` and exits 70 on any thread, so the status stays 70 under `panic = "abort"` (the default hook gives 134) | `in_workers` (`tools/test262/src/exec.rs:1278`), `install_panic_hook` (`tools/test262/src/exec.rs:1028`), `parse::worker` (`tools/test262/src/parse.rs:598`), `internal_error` (`crates/ep9_cli/src/run.rs:417`); tests `tools/test262/tests/infra_errors.rs`, `tools/test262/tests/exec.rs`, `crates/ep9_cli/tests/cli.rs` |
| Scope of `panic = "abort"` | One setting per profile for the whole build: Cargo rejects `panic` in a `[profile.*.package.<name>]` table ("`panic` may not be specified in a `package` profile"). A panic that leaves an `extern "C"` function aborts (exit 134), even under `catch_unwind` | measured 2026-10-21 with Rust 1.99.1 (M2-P1-ABORT-PRE) |
| Native stack past the margin (guard page) | Rust std prints "has overflowed its stack" and aborts (exit 134, SIGABRT, macOS arm64); no ep9 signal handler | Rust std; no `sigaction` in `crates/` |
| BigInt size (2026-11-05, spec-temporal B2) | 2^30 bits (`ep9_bigint::MAX_BITS`, V8's `kMaxLengthBits`): an operation whose result is larger throws RangeError "Maximum BigInt size exceeded", before the result cell is allocated. Every operator result and every `BigInt.asIntN` and `asUintN` result passes one check, `bigint::from_result` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/bigint.rs`), before its cell is allocated, and a string parse checks its result in `ep9_bigint`. Five also have an early guard that runs before the work. Two guards are exact: `<<` and `>>` by a negative count (bit length + shift count), and `asUintN` of a negative value (the bit count; exact for an input of at most 2^30 bits; `asUintN` has only this guard). Three guards are lower bounds, so some results above the limit are computed and then refused: `*` (sum of the bit lengths - 1; at most 1 bit over), `**` ((bit length - 1) x exponent; the bases 0n, 1n and -1n are exempt; exact when \|base\| is a power of 2; with base 3 it computes up to about 1.6 x 2^30 bits: `3n ** 1073741823n` runs 15 s before the RangeError) and a string parse ((digit count - 1) x floor(log2 radix); exact for radix 2 and 16; a decimal string computes up to about 1.11 x 2^30 bits: `BigInt` of 330,000,000 nines runs 50 s). Measured 2026-11-05, Apple M5 Max, release build. A literal of a unit is not checked (the loader cannot throw): a literal above 2^30 bits loads (up to 2^31 - 1 digits; a longer one panics at load, `alloc_literal`), and an operator, `asIntN` or `asUintN` result from it throws if the result is above 2^30 bits, also one that does not grow (`x / 1n`, `-x`, `x >> 0n`); a result that shrinks to at most 2^30 bits does not throw (`BigInt.asIntN(0, x)` and `BigInt.asUintN(0, x)` give 0n, also `x * 0n`, `x & 1n`, `x - x`) [2026-11-06, SPECINT-CODEX-FINAL]; `typeof`, a compare and `Number(x)` do not throw, and `String(x)` throws only the String length RangeError (`bigint::to_string`). BytecodeGen has no literal size check, so a source literal above the limit loads the same way [2026-11-06, spec-int fixer, Codex SPECINT-CODEX-DOCS F2]. `BigInt.prototype.toString` checks the String length row before it converts, from an upper bound of the length (bit length and radix) that can be 1 over, so it can refuse a text whose length is the limit (node gives that text) [2026-11-06, M7-P0 SPEC-POST (fill; MC-SPEC-BIGPOLL):] Two early guards act earlier now. `**` checks a lower bound of the result before each square (`pow_steps`, `crates/ep9_bigint/src/lib.rs:321`), so `3n ** 1073741823n` throws at once (0 ms; 19.5 s at the base). A parse checks (digit count - 1) x log2(radix) before the work, with a fixed-point lower bound of log2(radix) (`LOG2_RADIX`, `crates/ep9_bigint/src/lib.rs:660`; leading zeros not counted): `BigInt` of 323,228,498 or more decimal digits throws at once (330,000,000 nines: 226 ms, the string copy; 88 s at the base on a loaded host), and a parse that passes the guard computes at most 2^30 + 5 bits (323,228,497 nines: 53 s, with polls, then the RangeError). The `toString` length bound stays as it was (1 over for some lengths) [2026-11-06, M7-P0 fixer r3: the exact power-of-2 bound of aa5149df was reverted, M6-ORCH-145]. Measured 2026-11-06, Apple M5 Max, release builds of the M7-P0 base and head | `crates/ep9_bigint/src/lib.rs`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/bigint.rs`; `ep9_bigint` `tests/ops.rs` `size_limit_edges`; `ep9_builtins` `tests/bigint.rs` `bitwise_results_above_the_size_limit_are_range_errors`; `ep9_vm` `tests::bigint::an_oversized_literal_throws_on_every_operation`, `tests::bigint::a_unit_literal_above_the_limit_loads`; [2026-11-06, M7-P0 SPEC-POST:] `ep9_bigint` `tests/ops.rs` `pow_refuses_a_too_big_result_before_the_last_squares`, `a_parse_above_the_limit_is_refused_before_the_work` |
| Language tag length (2026-11-07, INTL W1 fixer r3; reviewer R1, M6-ORCH-212) [2026-11-07, INTL W1 fixer r4, Codex r2 F3 (M6-ORCH-225): on input strings only] | 65,536 code units (`ep9_intl::tag::MAX_TAG_LEN`) for each string that JS gives: a language tag (the String tag of `Intl.Locale`; a String argument or element of CanonicalizeLocaleList: `Intl.getCanonicalLocales`, the `Intl.Collator` locale list, `Intl.Collator.supportedLocalesOf`) and the value of each free-form `Intl.Locale` option (`variants`, `calendar`, `collation`, `numberingSystem`, `firstDayOfWeek`; the other options are short by their grammar). A longer tag is a RangeError "Incorrect locale information provided: a language tag of N code units is longer than the limit of 65536" (`TagError::TooLong`, a distinct error, so no caller can fall back to the input tag); a longer option value is a RangeError "Value of N code units for Intl.Locale options property P is longer than the limit of 65536" (`locale::limited_option`). Each is checked first, before the subtag split or the option grammar, so before any ICU4X call. A tag that the engine made from such inputs is not checked again (`tag::canonicalize_made_tag`, `locale::canonical_made_locale`): the `[[Locale]]` of an `Intl.Locale` (as the tag or as a list element), the new tag of UpdateLanguageId (the step throws only on option values), MakeLocaleRecord (it returns a Record), `maximize` and `minimize` (the spec uses `! Construct`, so they cannot throw the limit) and the ResolveLocale step InsertUnicodeExtensionAndCanonicalize. Such a tag is longer than its inputs only by alias values ("islamicc" -> "islamic-civil": +5 units) and by the option values (each replaces a part of the tag, it does not add to it): at most about 5 x 65,536 code units plus keys and aliases; measured 327,701 units (327,709 after `maximize`; the Codex r2 verifier's form: 327,698 and 327,706, 24-80 ms in a release build), and each subtag list stays at most 32,768 subtags, below the knee. A canonical tag given back as a String is a new input: `Intl.getCanonicalLocales(Intl.getCanonicalLocales(t)[0])`, with t of 65,536 units whose canonical form has 65,541, is a RangeError (node takes it; no spec text asks for a limit either way). No ECMA-402 text gives a limit; V8 and JavaScriptCore throw a RangeError through ICU4C limits (a 157-character base name, `ULOC_FULLNAME_CAPACITY`). Why: the icu_locale_core 2.3.0 subtag lists (`ShortBoxSlice` `push` and `insert`) reallocate per subtag, O(n^2) past about 120,000 subtags, inside one ICU4X call with no poll (320,000 subtags: from about 30 s in a debug build to more than 120 s in release, by list). At the limit each list takes at most 12 ms; a 320,000-subtag tag through the `Intl.Locale` options throws in 15-39 ms (release, load average 11). The longest test262 tag has 80 characters. Measured 2026-11-07, Apple M5 Max | `crates/ep9_intl/src/tag.rs` (`MAX_TAG_LEN`, `TagError`, `parse`, `canonicalize_made_tag`); `crates/ep9_intl/src/locale.rs` (`limited_option`, `canonical_made_locale`); `ep9_intl` `tests/tag_limit.rs` `a_tag_at_the_limit_works_and_one_unit_more_is_a_range_error`, `tags_that_the_engine_made_have_no_limit`, `a_320k_subtag_tag_through_the_options_path_ends_at_once` |

**Fills (2026-10-21).**

| Topic | Rule | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| Panic policy | Release builds unwind until the first JIT or AOT frame exists (Spike B). From then on shipped builds use `panic = "abort"`, and no panic unwinds through a JIT or AOT frame. Before that switch, ep9-test262 exec and parse, the front-end worker and the `ep9` exit-70 path stop relying on `catch_unwind` (for example a child process per shard) | W4-PANIC-ABORT |
| Out of memory | A near-limit callback runs first (the embedder may raise the limit); then a fatal abort with a message. Never a JS exception. Built in months 2-3 | W4-H23 |
| Size limits | String, array and buffer lengths past a limit throw RangeError; the isolate stays usable | W4-H23 |
| Interrupts | Embedder interrupt callbacks never run JS. Only an inspector session runs JS at a poll | W4-H23 |
| Guard pages | A native stack overflow past the margin is fatal (abort with a message). The margin checks exist so that JS never reaches the guard page. The handler, alternate stack and SEH mechanism belong to H28 (H28-Q3) | W4-H23 |
| Failed deopt (2026-10-29) | A deopt that cannot rebuild its VM frame (the deopt reader refuses the exit: no metadata for the code, no record at the exit's pc, a value the frame cannot take) is fatal: abort with the message `ep9: fatal: deopt at native offset <pc>: <why>` on stderr. Never a JS exception: a script's `catch` would hide an engine bug, and jitless has no native code, so it never reaches this point and a mode differential would see two outputs. As the out-of-memory row: an engine invariant broke, so no JS may observe it [2026-10-29, M5-P2 text fixer; MODES-CHECK-w20 MC-w20-1: replaces the reason "and jitless has no native code, so it never reaches this point and a mode differential would see two outputs", which is wrong. A jitless isolate runs native code too when it has unit code (AotBaseline, AotOpt; doc 13 §1.3), jitless AotOpt code deopts in budget (doc 13 §3.3 T5), and the deoptimizer builds without `jit` (doc 13 §3.4, C-10). The reasons are: a refused deopt means the deopt metadata does not match its code, an engine bug, and the VM frame cannot be rebuilt, so no JS state is right to go on from (to resume or to throw); a script's `catch`, or a fuzzer's try/catch, would hide the bug; and a failed deopt is never a valid outcome in any mode, so every isolate that runs native code (JIT, jitless with unit code, hybrid) aborts the same way, and no mode turns it into JS output that a mode differential could see. An isolate that runs no native code never gets here. Today the path builds only under `spike-native` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/lib.rs:102-103`)] [2026-11-08, M7-P3 integration fixer (clarify; MODES-CHECK-w33 MCV-w33-1, MCV-w33-2): the last sentence of the note above held until M7-P3 A2-ENTRY (d6b4651d); its cite names tag m7-p2 (a history pin). Since then the path builds in every build: `fatal_deopt` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/native.rs:1305`) is reached from `deopt_at` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/native.rs:1261`) through `rt_deopt` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/native.rs:476`), the `deopt` entry of `native::RUNTIME_TABLE`, which `Cx::runtime_table` points at in every build. The rule and its reasons do not change] | M5-P1-ORCH-12 (MODES-CHECK-w19 MC-w19-9) |
| JS-visible limits (2026-11-04) | A limit whose result JS can see is a count that every tier checks the same way, at the same step, so T0, JIT code, unit code and an inlined builtin give the same result for the same input. Today these are the string length, the dense array length, the front-end depth caps, the proxy forward depth and hop count, the BigInt size [2026-11-05, spec-temporal B2], the language tag length [2026-11-07, INTL W1 fixer r3, reviewer R1] (Facts), and the depth caps of the recursive builtins (`MAX_DEPTH` of `JSON.stringify` and the `JSON.parse` reviver, `crates/ep9_builtins/src/json.rs`; `MAX_FLATTEN_DEPTH` of `flat` and `flatMap`, `crates/ep9_builtins/src/array.rs`). The native-stack check stays a backstop: it throws first only when the thread's stack is too small for the count (a small embedder thread), and it keeps every overflow a catchable RangeError in every tier. The depth of a plain recursion is not such a limit, because frame sizes differ by tier (T0 104,855 levels of `function f() { f() }`, Facts; T0 and Spike B JIT code 7,901 to 9,961 levels, H23-Q5). A RegExp compiled by RX2 that reaches a limit of its own (code size, backtrack stack) falls back to the RegExp interpreter for that exec, never a new RangeError | M6-P3 B3T2 (MODES-CHECK-w21 MC-w21-17; plan v11 inputs item 32) |
| Budget units and polls (2026-11-04) | Two counts, named. (1) The **interrupt budget** counts every charge of the Facts row "Interrupt poll" (calls, back edges, bound levels, lookup contexts, builtin loop passes, string search work, jobs, cleanup callbacks, walk steps); a poll runs when it runs out. A tier that runs a loop or walk that T0 charges takes the same charge and polls when the budget runs out; a walk that compiled code removes (a guard stands in for it) takes no unit. Which work T0 charges, and how many units, is a T0 fact as built: the counts of the Facts row, held by the Freeze-table part "tuning (gated)". (2) The **tier unit** is one unit per call and one per `JumpLoop` back edge taken, wherever the poll stands. "The H23 budget unit" of H33 (the Q1 budget word and the Q5 rule) and of doc 13 (F2, C-08) is the tier unit: a compiled tier that counts hotness counts tier units, never library work. As built, T0 keeps one counter for both, so a library walk also shortens the sampled-call window (`CALL_WINDOW` counts interrupt budget units, `crates/ep9_vm/src/profile.rs:97-110`; an H16 tuning value): a T0 fact, not a rule for later tiers. (3) A loop in compiled code polls at each back edge, or once per stretch of at most N turns, or, with a static trip bound, once at loop entry; each form charges the turns it covers, so the units equal the back edges taken. A bound that is only a data length is not a static bound. A poll writes no program state and may collect; while an inspector session is connected, T2 and AOT code treat it as a heap effect point (H24) | M6-P3 B3T2 (MODES-CHECK-w16 MC-w16-7, MODES-CHECK-w17 MC-w17-13) |

## Rules

| Rule | Check | From |
|---|---|---|
| VM-stack and native-stack overflow give a catchable RangeError; the isolate stays usable | `vm_stack_overflow_is_a_catchable_range_error`, `native_stack_overflow_is_a_catchable_range_error` (every dispatch kind) | M0 wk3 |
| Every nesting form at depth cap + 1 gives RangeError, never an abort | `every_form_is_gated` (`crates/ep9_frontend/src/depth_tests.rs:229`) | M0 wk3 |
| Termination is uncatchable, runs no `finally`, and leaves the isolate usable | `terminate_from_another_thread` | M0 wk3 |
| A panic, timeout or worker crash in ep9-test262 is an infrastructure error (exit 2), never a pass or a fail | decision T262-INFRA; `EP9_TEST262_FAULT` hooks | M0 wk3 |
| JIT and AOT prologues check the stack limit | Spike B; A2 gate | Spike B |
| No panic unwinds through a JIT or AOT frame | `panic = "abort"` in `[profile.release]` and no other profile setting `panic`: dep-guards check 13 (CI job `dependency guards`). The spike-b workspace sets its own `[profile.release] panic = "abort"` | Spike B (live since M0 month 2 P2) |
| The out-of-memory path calls the near-limit callback before it aborts | `the_near_limit_callback_raises_the_heap_limit_once`, `a_kept_limit_is_the_fatal_heap_limit_panic` (`ep9_vm` `tests::isolate_api`); `a_raised_limit_lets_the_allocation_retry`, `a_kept_limit_gives_out_of_memory_after_one_call`, `alloc_shape_calls_it_too` (`ep9_heap`, both allocation entries) | M0 months 2-3 |
| An embedder interrupt callback runs once, on the isolate thread, at the next poll, and runs no JS | `a_requested_callback_runs_at_the_first_poll` (every dispatch, debug and release), `a_callback_reaches_a_running_endless_loop`, `a_callback_waits_for_a_poll_and_callbacks_keep_their_order` (`ep9_vm` `tests::isolate_api`); no `Cx` in the callback type | M0 month 2 P3 |
| A deopt that the side table refuses aborts the process with its message; no JS exception (Fills: Failed deopt) | `a_deopt_the_side_table_refuses_aborts` (`ep9_vm` `tests::native`, `spike-native`, aarch64: two child processes end by SIGABRT with the message: code with no metadata, a pc with no record) | M5-P2 |

## Consequences

- **Gains.** Hostile input gives catchable errors (G6). ep9 recurses 10x deeper than node, so recursion that works on node works on ep9. Termination needs no signal.
- **Costs.** Since the switch (M0 month 2 P2), a panic in a release build ends the process: a test262 panic is a `worker crash` with the panic text from the worker's stderr (crash isolation per file, M2-P1-ABORT-PRE), and `ep9` exits 70 through its panic hook. An abort on Windows exits with 0xC0000409. No default heap limit below the cage (H23-Q3): a runaway script uses up to the 64 GiB cage unless the embedder sets `max_heap_bytes`. An interrupt callback waits for the next poll: up to one budget (10,000 back edges, bytecode calls or other budget units) while JS runs, and until the next run when the isolate is idle. A call of a host function that is not bound takes no budget unit, so code that only calls such functions does not poll: for example, straight-line code of host calls, or an embedder loop of `Cx::call` on a host function (in `Isolate::with_cx`; the raw `Isolate::call` until M3-P3). The callback and a termination then wait for the next bytecode call, back edge, bound-function level or builtin poll. A call of a bound function takes one unit per bound level, also when its target is a host function (Facts, Interrupt poll).
- **Follow-ups.** H28-Q3 handler; each recursive builtin calls `Cx::check_native_stack` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/index.rs:107`) at each recursion step when it lands (from week 4; the first is FlattenIntoArray, M0 month 2 P4). Done: the near-limit callback and the embedder interrupt callback (M0 month 2 P3), prologue checks (Spike B, H23-Q5).

## Evidence

| Tag | Claim used here | Link |

| [R09] | Stack overflow, OOM, termination and panic policy constrain early design; JS recursion must throw RangeError; tsrs and oxc abort on deep nesting | [09-critic.md](../research/09-critic.md) |
| [R18] | A worker inspector session's `Runtime.evaluate` ran inside a call-free loop and changed a field type: every interrupt poll is a lazy-deopt point | [18-sound-regions.md](../research/18-sound-regions.md) |
| [P1] | Plan v1 depth probe: 20k levels need about 40 MB of stack (01 §6) | Session-local |
| [M] | Recursion depths above; Rust std guard-page abort (exit 134) | This ADR, measured 2026-10-21 |

## Validation

- Now: the tests above on 4 CI targets; the week-3 depth check; test262 exec reports 0 infrastructure errors (CI run 39518270586).
- Spike B: JIT prologue checks and the abort rule. A2: AOT prologues.

## Open questions

| ID | Question | Needed by | Proposed default |
|---|---|---|---|
| H23-Q1 | When does `panic = "abort"` start? | Spike B | **Filled 2026-10-21: from the first JIT or AOT frame (Spike B)** (W4-PANIC-ABORT) |
| H23-Q2 | What does out of memory do? | M0 months 2-3 | **Filled 2026-10-21: near-limit callback, then fatal abort** (W4-H23) |
| H23-Q3 | Default heap limit | M0 months 2-3 | **Filled 2026-10-22: no default below the cage.** `HeapConfig::max_heap_bytes` stays `usize::MAX`, so the 64 GiB per-isolate cage bounds the heap; an embedder that wants a limit sets `max_heap_bytes` and `near_limit`. Measured: node 24.22.1 has `heap_size_limit` 4,496,293,888 B (4,288 MiB) on a 128 GiB Apple M5 Max (`v8.getHeapStatistics()`); V8 sizes it from physical memory for its generational old space, which ep9 does not have. A lower default would turn a program that fits in memory into a fatal abort with no JS exception (H23-Q2). Check: `the_default_heap_limit_is_the_cage` (M2-P3-H23-Q3) |
| H23-Q4 | VM stack default | M1 | Keep 2^20 slots (H14-Q4). **Filled 2026-11-04 (M6-P3 B3T2; FREEZE-M1 B3): kept, as built:** 2^20 slots (8 MiB) by default, 1024..=2^28 accepted (Facts row "VM register stack"; `crates/ep9_vm/src/isolate.rs:86`). The number is in the Freeze-table part "tuning (gated)" (A4 gate), so a retune with test262 and bench data (H14-Q4) is not a G8 count |
| H23-Q5 | Form of the JIT and AOT prologue check | Spike B | **Filled by Spike B (M2-P2).** The JIT and AOT prologue check is one unsigned compare of sp with the per-isolate limit Cx::native_limit (layout::CX_NATIVE_LIMIT; the value of Runtime::native_limit, stack low + clamp(size/4, 128 KiB, 1 MiB)). AArch64: ldr x16, [x28, #native_limit]; mov x17, sp; cmp x17, x16; b.ls overflow. x86-64: cmp rsp, [r15 + native_limit]; jbe overflow. At or below the limit the prologue calls RuntimeTable::stack_overflow(cx, fp, 0), a catchable RangeError, and returns 0. Entries into native code from T0 and Rust (call_any, call_from_rust) check Runtime::native_stack_ok first. Evidence: spike-b gate check (f), T0<->JIT recursion ends in a catchable RangeError at depth 9007 (816 B/level, mac a64), 9961 (736 B/level, linux a64), 7901 (928 B/level, linux x64); tests::native::deep_t0_native_recursion_is_a_catchable_range_error |
| H23-Q6 | Embedder interrupt API | M1 | A callback at a poll; **filled: it never runs JS** (W4-H23). **Built 2026-10-22 (M0 month 2 P3):** `Isolate::interrupt_handle()` gives a `Send + Sync + Clone` `InterruptHandle`; `request(Box<dyn FnOnce() + Send>)` queues a callback that runs once, on the isolate thread, at the next poll (interrupt bit `REQUEST` = 2). It gets no `Cx`, so it runs no JS and allocates nothing on the JS heap; it may terminate. A callback still queued when the isolate is dropped is dropped with it, never run, and a request after that drops its callback at once (the handle holds the queue weakly, so a callback that holds a handle makes no cycle; P3 review ISO-CYCLE) |
| H23-Q7 | Are polls GC safepoints? | S2 | Yes (02 §6 row) |
| H23-Q8 | Termination details: cancel, re-entry | M1 | As V8: uncatchable, skips `finally`, the isolate is reusable (as built). **Filled 2026-11-04 (M6-P3 B3T2; FREEZE-M1 B3): as built.** A termination runs no `catch` and no `finally`. The end of the terminated run drops the job queue and aborts the async modules that waited for it (`end_termination`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/isolate.rs:895`). Re-entry: the isolate stays usable, and its next run, call or checkpoint runs normally (`terminate_from_another_thread`, `a_terminated_run_or_call_drops_its_queued_jobs`). Cancel: there is none; a request through `TerminateHandle::terminate` cannot be withdrawn (V8's `CancelTerminateExecution` has no ep9 form). A cancel call is a fill when an embedder needs one |
| H23-Q9 | Native stack overflow past the margin | M0 wk4 | **Filled 2026-10-21: fatal; mechanism in H28-Q3** (W4-H23) |
| H23-Q10 | oxc layer-1 depth counter | M1 | Upstream patch first (01 §7). **Filled 2026-11-04 (M6-P3 B3T2; FREEZE-M1 B3): no layer-1 counter.** The depth limit stays on layers 0, 2 and 3 (`AST_CAP`, `PRESCAN_CAP`; the Rules row "Every nesting form at depth cap + 1 gives RangeError"), as 01 §7 gate P1 reads it: a layer-1 oxc counter becomes a local patch only if a counted front-end cargo-fuzz run finds a depth miss. Counted run 1 found none (`frontend_compile` 8.05 CPU-h, 0 crashes; DL M6-ORCH-9); each later counted run is read the same way |

## Freeze table

README §3.4 form (the Freeze point field above; plan v11 §5.7, M6-PLAN-D26; doc 13 C-25 asks the H23 part). Part names hold no `,` and no ` and `, because the Frozen check reads a Status line that lists several parts (`tools/xtask/src/exit/freeze.rs`).

| Part | What it holds | Freeze point | After the freeze point | Check |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| rules | Every row of this ADR except the tuning numbers: the Decision; the Facts rows as the forms they state (which events take units and poll, each limit and its error, the panic and out-of-memory policy); the Fills, with "JS-visible limits" and "Budget units and polls"; the Rules rows; the open-question fills | M1 exit | Frozen-part rules (README §3.2) | the Rules rows |
| tuning (gated) | The T0 numbers that set a size or a count: the interrupt budget (10,000 units, `BUDGET`); the unit count of each charge of the Facts row "Interrupt poll" (the instanceof walk group of 8 included, MODES-CHECK-w16 MC-w16-5; the 256 code units of a string search step); the native margin (size / 4, clamped to 128 KiB..1 MiB) and the 64 KiB debug margin; the VM register stack default (2^20 slots, H23-Q4) and its accepted range; the CLI thread stack (64 MiB); the dense array length (2^26, v0); and the values of the count limits of the Fills row "JS-visible limits" (`AST_CAP`, `PRESCAN_CAP`, `MAX_DEPTH`, `MAX_FLATTEN_DEPTH`, `MAX_FORWARD_DEPTH`, `MAX_PROXY_HOPS`, `MAX_BITS` [2026-11-06, spec-int fixer, checker F5: spec-temporal B2, the H23 2026-11-06 fill row], `MAX_TAG_LEN` [2026-11-07, INTL W1 fixer r3: reviewer R1, the H23 2026-11-07 fill row]). A new value keeps its rule: a JS-visible limit stays a count that every tier checks the same way | the A4 gate (M8 exit) | Frozen-part rules (README §3.2) | BENCH-SET and the depth tests at each retune |

## Reopens if

- A tier-1 target cannot keep a catchable RangeError for VM or native stack overflow.
- Spike B shows that a JIT frame cannot run with the abort rule (for example an unwinder needs panics).
- D14 changes (11-decisions "Affects": H11, H22, H23).

Checked at 3749f9b; `call.rs` and `native.rs` did not change after cbf4208 (`git diff cbf4208 3749f9b` on both is empty). (1) `call_bytecode` (the bytecode part of `call::call_function_object`, which runs it after `realm::enter_realm_of`): `poll`, `try_bind_this`, `push_frame`, then `cx.budget -= 1` (`call.rs` line 496), then the push check (`let Some(fp) = fp else { return stack_overflow(cx) }`). So the unit comes before the push check, and a callee of another realm takes it 'at the frame push' (was 'after'). (2) Under spike-native, `run_b` checks the native stack, enters the callee's realm and calls `run_b_in_realm`, which pushes the frame, then takes the unit (`native.rs` line 1209), then checks the push. The callers of `run_b` are `b_from_rust` (from `call_from_rust`), the (b) branch of `call_any` and `call_in_its_realm`: the three that the text names. (3) ed33646 moved each decrement from after the push check (and after `wrap_this` / `wrap_receiver`) to before it: the 'Before, ... only after a push that succeeded' sentence is true, and the removed 0-unit item (a callee of another realm when the VM stack is full) is false now, so its removal is right. (4) Tests: `a_call_from_rust_that_does_not_fit_takes_one_unit` (every `Dispatch::ALL` expansion; `call_raw` with no room for a frame, at budgets 1, 2 and 10, leaves `budget - 1`; a call that fits takes 1) and, under spike-native, `a_b_call_from_rust_that_does_not_fit_takes_one_unit` (`native::call_from_rust` of a (b) function), in `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/ic.rs` (`tests::ic`), as the text says. Note (c), no fix: a native-stack overflow (`native_stack_ok` in `call_function_object` and in `run_b`) still returns before the decrement and takes 0 units; the text speaks only of a full VM stack, and its general rule (a call that throws earlier takes no unit for the step it did not reach) covers this case. (1) The Facts row 'FinalizationRegistry cleanup drain' replaces 'cleanup rounds'. Code as in the H20 fixer C rewrite row 1: the checkpoint goes round again while cleanup is pending, with no cap; `poll_interrupt` before each callback; `set_pending` on a poll or callback `Err`. Its refs read: abi/weakref.rs line 227 is the poll, abi/jobs.rs line 247 is `if rt.kept_objects.is_pending() {`. Its three named tests pass (debug and `--release`). `MAX_CLEANUP_ROUNDS` is gone from H23's Facts (only dated rows name it). (2) The 'Interrupt poll' note: one unit per cleanup callback (`Cx::poll_interrupt`, abi/index.rs: `budget -= 1`, a poll at 0 or below) and per `prototype_walk_step` (property.rs: the same, then the step; used by `ordinary_has_instance_of_function`, `for_in_keys` and `Cx::prototype_walk_step`). The hit-walk text (as 319f303 left it) is true: `instance_ic::hit` walks in groups of `WALK_STEPS` (8): the inner loop returns TRUE (a match), FALSE (the end of the chain) or a miss (a proxy) with no unit, and only after a full group does `*budget -= WALK_STEPS as i64` run, with a miss when the budget is at or below 0. So a walk of 8 steps or fewer takes no unit, the last 1 to 8 steps of a longer walk take none, a walk that uses up a budget of 1 or more runs at most 7 steps past it, and one that starts at 0 or below runs 8 steps (`call_bytecode` in call.rs takes its unit at line 496, after its `poll` at line 470, so a callee can start at 0). A miss goes to the cold path: `property::instance_of` reaches `prototype_walk_step`, which takes a unit and polls at 0 or below at its first step. Line 496 of call.rs is `cx.budget -= 1;`. Plan: P4-ORCH-8 (remove `MAX_CLEANUP_ROUNDS` and its Facts row, the budget rule instead): done; B1-1 (fixC-inputs: bound or poll the IC walk, a regression on a warm site, measure): done in af13f96 (the test; wb-inst-mono +1.17%, BENCH-SET within 0.2% against 930945e). Wording note, (c), not in this set: the H23 walk Facts row (fixer B) says each step of a walk '(instanceof, ...)' takes one unit; that is `prototype_walk_step`, and the cache's hit walk exception is in the note above. Kind rewrite (proposed; Accepted path): agree: a decided limit goes away and a Facts row is replaced. (1) Text: the word diff of 29a095c on H23 adds one dated bracket clause in the Facts row 'Interrupt poll' (H23:33), after the fixer C bracket, and this Change-log row; no other body text changes (281b843 adds row 4 only). The clause labels the group size 8 (`WALK_STEPS`) a speed value and a tuning value in the doc 13 C-25 form (A4 gate) and states the rule. Code: `instance_ic::hit` walks in groups of `WALK_STEPS` (`const WALK_STEPS: usize = 8;` in `crates/ep9_vm/src/instance_ic.rs`, unchanged since 4941d6a; af8c9e9 changes comments in other files only). The inner loop returns TRUE (a match), FALSE (the chain's end) or a miss (a proxy) with no unit; only after a full group does `*budget -= WALK_STEPS as i64` run, with a miss at or below 0. So at most one group of a walk (its last 1 to 8 steps) goes uncharged, and a walk that uses up the budget misses after at most one group past it; the miss goes to `property::instance_of`, whose generic walk (`prototype_walk_step`) takes a unit and polls at its first step. The rule the text states holds at 281b843; the value and the code are those of 4941d6a. The fixer C bracket before it (unchanged) still gives the exact counts: no unit for the last 1 to 8 steps, up to 7 steps past the budget, 8 from 0 or below. (2) w16.md MC-w16-5 Fix ('one clause in both ...'): done in H23:33, the same clause as the H16-Q3 note. (3) R-P5M-1 [low] (b), a wording defect of the new text: 'at most one group of uncharged steps per walk (8 today), then the generic walk polls' joins two facts with 'then'. The uncharged group is the last group of a walk that returns (a match or the chain's end: no generic walk follows) or misses on a proxy; the generic walk polls after a budget miss, which comes after a charged group. Suggested text: 'at most one group of steps per walk goes uncharged (8 today), and a walk that uses up the budget misses after at most one group past it, so the generic walk polls'. w16's Fix wording has the same 'then'. Reported to the orchestrator; not changed here (a records reviewer writes record rows only). In `instance_ic::hit` the inner loop of `WALK_STEPS` (8) steps returns TRUE (a match), FALSE (the chain's end) or a miss (a proxy) with no unit. Only after a full group does it take 8 units, and it misses at or below 0. So at most one group per walk goes uncharged: the last 1 to 8 steps of a walk that returns. A budget miss comes after a charged group, at most one group past the point where the budget ran out (8 steps when the budget is at or below 0 at entry: a call takes its unit after its poll, `call::call_bytecode`, so it can leave 0). The miss goes cold to `property::instance_of`. Its walk calls `prototype_walk_step`, which takes 1 unit and polls when the budget is at or below 0, so it polls at its first step. The new text is true at 6ebe361. The fixer C bracket just before the clause (unchanged) gives the exact counts, and they agree with it. The SPECINT-CODEX-FINAL finding 'Qualify the oversized-literal rejection guarantee' (docs slice d1, H23 at line 48) is on this row; a verifier found it true, sort (b) (this branch added the Facts row and the F2 note; neither is in the m1-exit text 3bdaf59e). The text said that the first operator, `asIntN` or `asUintN` result from a unit literal above 2^30 bits throws. I read the code first: `bigint::from_result` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/bigint.rs:105`) refuses a result only if it is above `MAX_BITS`; `as_int_n` and `as_uint_n` give 0n for 0 bits (`crates/ep9_bigint/src/lib.rs:588`, `crates/ep9_bigint/src/lib.rs:621`), `mul` gives 0n for a 0n side before its guard (`crates/ep9_bigint/src/lib.rs:190`), and `as_n` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/bigint.rs:332`) does no check on the input. So a result that shrinks to at most 2^30 bits is a value. The code is correct (in V8 no BigInt above the limit can exist); the text said too much. Fixed in place, no line moves, in the commit of this row ("docs/adr: spec-int Codex final fixes"): (1) H23:48: the result throws "if the result is above 2^30 bits", and a result that shrinks does not throw (`BigInt.asIntN(0, x)`, `BigInt.asUintN(0, x)`, `x * 0n`, `x & 1n`, `x - x` give 0n); (2) the F2 note of H23:212: "throw too when the result stays above the limit"; (3) a dated note in the round-1 row 3 of this set marks its "names exactly" clause not exact. Test e768c06a ("ep9_vm: test a shrinking result of an oversized BigInt literal") adds `an_oversized_literal_gives_a_shrinking_result` at the end of `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/bigint.rs` (no cited line moves): `asIntN` and `asUintN` of 0 and 64 bits, `x * 0n`, `x & 1n` and `x - x` of a literal of 2^30 + 1 bits are 0n; it changes the doc comment of `an_oversized_literal_throws_on_every_operation` to "each operation whose result stays above the limit" (same lines). `cargo test --locked -p ep9_vm --lib tests::bigint::`: 14 pass, 0 fail (my run).
